# Decision Log — shipwright

_This log was bootstrapped from existing prior art at `README.md#architecture` — see the **Imported decisions** section below for the verbatim contents._

### ADR-009: Adopt this repository into the Shipwright SDLC

- **Status**: accepted
- **Date**: 2026-05-02
- **Commit**: `87dbf72a8614bcf85ab28c2793517800fa907450`

#### Context

This repository existed with 0 detected feature(s) and substantive git history before /shipwright-adopt ran. The goal is to bring it under the Shipwright SDLC (CLAUDE.md + .shipwright/agent_docs + .shipwright/planning/ + .shipwright/compliance/ + configs) without disrupting the existing codebase.

#### Decision

Adopted into Shipwright using profile `python-plugin-monorepo` and scope `library`. Retroactively marked `completed_steps = ["project", "plan", "build", "test"]` so that `/shipwright-iterate` and downstream skills (`/shipwright-compliance`, `/shipwright-test`) work as on a natively-built project.

#### Consequences

- Future changes MUST go through `/shipwright-iterate` (not `/shipwright-project`/`/shipwright-plan`/`/shipwright-build`).
- Compliance reports (RTM, SBOM, change-history) are seeded; test-evidence starts collecting from the first `/shipwright-test` run.
- Any existing E2E baseline auto-generated by Adopt (under `e2e/flows/adopted-baseline.spec.ts`) is a regression guard, not a substitute for real acceptance tests.

#### Rejected alternatives

- Manual `/shipwright-project` init: would lose git history and force re-description of existing code.
- No adoption (ad-hoc `/shipwright-iterate`): would mean missing configs, no compliance reports, and the audit pipeline would silently no-op.

---

### ADR-010: Plugin-per-phase architecture

- **Status**: accepted (retroactive, llm-inferred)
- **Commit**: ``

#### Context
Shipwright spans many distinct SDLC phases (project, plan, build, test, security, deploy, changelog, compliance, iterate, design, preview, adopt). A monolithic skill would have grown to thousands of lines and entangled phase-specific dependencies, hooks, and review chains.

#### Decision
Each SDLC phase is implemented as its own Claude Code plugin under plugins/<phase>/ with isolated pyproject.toml, hooks/hooks.json, skills/<phase>/SKILL.md, and tests. Cross-plugin code lives under shared/.

#### Consequences
Plugins evolve independently and have small surface area. The trade-off is a runtime plugin cache (~/.claude/plugins/cache/shipwright/) that must be re-synced after plugin-side changes via scripts/update-marketplace.sh. Hooks and the event log carry inter-plugin state.

---

### ADR-011: Hooks as single source of truth for between-phase actions

- **Status**: accepted (retroactive, llm-inferred)
- **Commit**: ``

#### Context
Between-phase quality gates (canon checks, drift checks, phase-quality stop hook, compliance updates) need to execute deterministically regardless of which plugin invoked the next phase.

#### Decision
All between-phase actions and quality gates are wired in plugins/<phase>/hooks/hooks.json and centrally documented in docs/hooks-and-pipeline.md. SKILL.md files do not duplicate hook behavior in prose; they reference the doc.

#### Consequences
Adding or changing a phase requires updating both hooks.json and docs/hooks-and-pipeline.md; the doc is the authoritative reference for who reads/writes which artifact and when each gate fires.

---

### ADR-012: External LLM review at SDLC gates (plan/iterate/code)

- **Status**: accepted (retroactive, llm-inferred)
- **Commit**: ``

#### Context
Self-review by the same LLM that wrote the plan or code has a strong confirmation bias. Single-reviewer dual-LLM checks (Gemini + GPT in parallel) caught real blockers (e.g. monorepo-auto-descent-guard semantics) that would otherwise have shipped invisible.

#### Decision
All external LLM review flows through one shared CLI: shared/scripts/tools/external_review.py, with three modes: plan (full plan vs project spec), iterate (mini-plan vs iterate spec), code (diff vs section/iterate spec). Each mode runs Gemini + GPT in parallel via OpenRouter and writes a marker file used by compliance.

#### Consequences
Non-trivial plans gain ~60s + cents-per-review of overhead in exchange for catching blocker-class bugs early. Marker files (external_review_state.json, external_code_review_state.json) are committed as compliance evidence. The underlying lib (llm_review.py) is implementation detail; skills must call the CLI.

---

### ADR-013: Per-scanner exclude lists for security tools

- **Status**: accepted (retroactive, llm-inferred)
- **Commit**: ``

#### Context
Trivy and Gitleaks do not honor .gitignore by default, so secrets stored in fixture or test directories were being flagged as production findings. Semgrep does honor gitignore.

#### Decision
Each scanner gets its own exclude list rather than collapsing on a single gitignore-as-SSoT model. .worktrees/ was added after a benchmark showed scan times improved.

#### Consequences
Slightly more configuration per scanner, but findings now match developer intent. Replaces the original "gitignore is SSoT for all scanners" thesis from earlier iterates.

---

### ADR-014: Artifact relocation under .shipwright/

- **Status**: accepted (retroactive, llm-inferred)
- **Commit**: ``

#### Context
Top-level directories named planning/, designs/, agent_docs/, and compliance/ collided with target-project directories of the same names and made it hard to tell which artifacts belonged to Shipwright vs the user's app.

#### Decision
Relocated all four artifact roots under .shipwright/<artifact>/ (planning, designs, agent_docs, compliance). Hard-cutover migration with sub-iterates A through G per artifact, plus a per-scanner gitignore-exclude pass (sub-iterate H). Documented as the standard pattern in docs/migrations/artifact-migration-reference.md.

#### Consequences
All Shipwright-owned artifacts live under .shipwright/. e2e/ stayed top-level (Playwright convention) and CHANGELOG-unreleased.d/ stayed top-level (Keep-a-Changelog sibling-to-CHANGELOG convention). Configuration JSON files (shipwright_*_config.json) and events.jsonl stayed top-level; their relocation was evaluated and deferred.

---

### ADR-015: /shipwright-iterate as primary post-adoption interface

- **Status**: accepted (retroactive, llm-inferred)
- **Commit**: ``

#### Context
Once a project is adopted into Shipwright, users still need a low-friction way to make code changes — fixes, small features, refactors — without invoking the full project/plan/build/test/deploy chain.

#### Decision
/shipwright-iterate is the canonical entry point for ongoing changes in any completed Shipwright project. It is complexity-adaptive: trivial changes proceed directly while medium-or-larger iterates run mini-planning, external review, TDD, and a structured review cascade. /shipwright-adopt installs the suggest_iterate hook to route ad-hoc change prompts to it.

#### Consequences
Users do not need to remember which SDLC phase to invoke for a given change. The trade-off is that /shipwright-iterate has substantial internal logic (intent + complexity classifier, sub-iterate spawning) that the other phases delegate to.

---

### ADR-016: Public-ready git history rewrite

- **Status**: accepted (retroactive, llm-inferred)
- **Commit**: ``

#### Context
The repo started with the maintainer's company email in commit metadata. Going public required cleaning that without losing history.

#### Decision
On 2026-04-30 the entire history (524 commits) was retroactively rewritten to use the GitHub-noreply email, then force-pushed. Documented as a one-time migration; future commits use the noreply email by default.

#### Consequences
Commit SHAs changed for every commit. External clones and forks made before that date no longer share history. Worth it for public-readiness.

---

## Imported decisions (verbatim from `README.md#architecture`)

_Copied during /shipwright-adopt onboarding. Original ADR numbering and ordering preserved. Future decisions land above this section, not within it._

## Architecture

```
shipwright/
├── plugins/                          # Claude Code plugins (one per SDLC phase)
│   ├── shipwright-run/               # Pipeline Initializer
│   ├── shipwright-project/           # Requirements decomposition (IREB)
│   ├── shipwright-design/            # UI mockups (HTML)
│   ├── shipwright-plan/              # Deep planning + external LLM review
│   ├── shipwright-build/             # TDD implementation
│   ├── shipwright-test/              # Test runner (unit/smoke/E2E)
│   ├── shipwright-security/          # Scanners + remediation
│   ├── shipwright-deploy/            # Deployment (extensible flavors)
│   ├── shipwright-changelog/         # Changelog + PR
│   ├── shipwright-compliance/        # Traceability, RTM, SBOM, dashboard
│   ├── shipwright-iterate/           # Daily iteration (complexity-adaptive)
│   ├── shipwright-preview/           # Local browser preview
│   └── shipwright-adopt/             # Brownfield onboarding (analyze existing repos)

---
```

### ADR-017: Repo cleanup post self-adoption: webui drift, legacy plans, FR populate
- **Date:** 2026-05-02
- **Section:** Iterate — change: post-adoption framework cleanup
- **Context:** Audit of the 2026-05-02 self-adoption surfaced 6 follow-up findings: stale webui sibling-directory references in CONTRIBUTING.md harvested into conventions.md, untracked legacy iterate plans, placeholder FR in spec.md, test-fixture noise in known_issues.md.
- **Decision:** Manual cleanup only (no plugin code changes): rewrite CONTRIBUTING.md to point at separate shipwright-webui repo, re-generate conventions.md from clean source, commit legacy iterate plans in-place under .shipwright/planning/iterate/ with README, populate spec.md FR-01.01..FR-01.13 from enrichment.json, add disclaimer header to known_issues.md, update test_profile_matcher to expect python-plugin-monorepo match.
- **Commit:** pending
- **Rationale:** Re-generating adopt artifacts is safer than manual conventions.md edit because preserve_existing.py:22-23 documents that agent_docs are overwritten on re-run; manual edits would be silently overwritten next time anyone re-runs adopt.
- **Consequences:** Repo state is now drift-free; 13 FRs available for RTM/compliance; 225 adopt tests green. Iterate 2 (adopt plugin durable fixes) is the second half of the post-adoption cleanup epic and addresses the framework-side root causes.
- **Rejected:** Touch adopt plugin in this iterate (deferred to Iterate 2 with external review); invent a _archive/ directory pattern (no framework backing, kept iterate plans in-place).

---

### ADR-018: Adopt plugin: drift detection, test-fixture filter, compliance fallback fix
- **Date:** 2026-05-02
- **Section:** Iterate — change: shipwright-adopt durable fixes (Iterate 2 of 2)
- **Context:** Self-adoption audit (2026-05-02) surfaced 3 bug classes in the shipwright-adopt plugin that produced silent-drift artifacts: prior_art_harvester verbatim-copied stale CONTRIBUTING.md path refs, known_issues inventory included test-fixture TODOs as real findings, and compliance_bridge fallback path failed for 3 of 5 generators with ModuleNotFoundError.
- **Decision:** Sub-2A: harvest_conventions(project_root, excludes=None) signature, default-None preserves backwards-compat. Post-harvest validation scans cd <path> tokens in fenced code blocks; excluded or absent paths get a <!-- adopt-drift: ... --> marker prepended to the block. Sub-2B: known_issues TODO scanner default-skips test-shaped paths (tests/ anywhere, test_*.py, *_test.py, *.test.ts, *.spec.ts) via _is_test_fixture_path heuristic; opt-in via scan_tests=True. Sub-2C: compliance_bridge.run_lib_fallback now also inserts lib_dir.parent.parent (= compliance plugin root) on sys.path so generators that do from scripts.lib.X import Y can resolve the scripts namespace package.
- **Commit:** pending
- **Rationale:** Sub-2A annotate-not-strip preserves harvested content while making drift visible to operators. Sub-2B default-skip is safe because broad grep over plugins/*/tests/ at audit time found 0 real workflow TODOs in plugin test directories; --scan-tests opt-in handles repos that legitimately track TODOs in tests. Sub-2C the sys.path fix is more chirurgical than refactoring relative imports across the compliance plugin; lives only in the adopt plugin and avoids cross-plugin coupling.
- **Consequences:** Future adoptions of brownfield repos with stale sibling-directory references in CONTRIBUTING.md will see drift markers in the generated conventions.md instead of inheriting the drift silently. known_issues.md no longer drowns real TODOs in test fixtures by default. compliance_bridge fallback is now consistent across all 5 generators. 233 adopt tests green (225 prior + 8 new TDD). External LLM iterate review skipped because the plan-level review (Plan v2-v3) already covered all sub-iterates.
- **Rejected:** Strip drift sections (too aggressive — verbatim-with-marker is non-destructive). Hard-skip tests/ everywhere (no opt-in escape would penalize repos with legitimate test TODOs). Refactor compliance generators to relative imports (cross-plugin churn for a 1-line fix in adopt).

---

### ADR-019: Hook installer writes canonical matcher-group shape
- **Date:** 2026-05-02
- **Section:** Iterate — bug: hook-installer-shape
- **Context:** /shipwright-adopt installed the UserPromptSubmit hook in the legacy bare-command shape (top-level type/command keys); Claude Code's parser requires the canonical matcher-group shape with an inner hooks array. The shipwright monorepo's self-adopt produced a settings.json that Claude Code rejected with 'Expected array, but received undefined', killing all permissions and hooks for the project.
- **Decision:** hook_installer.py now appends a matcher-group entry ({hooks:[{type,command}]}) instead of a bare command. The reader keeps both-shape detection for idempotency against settings.json files written by buggy pre-fix releases.
- **Commit:** PENDING
- **Rationale:** All plugin hooks.json files already use the canonical shape; the project-level installer was the lone outlier.
- **Consequences:** Future /shipwright-adopt runs produce parseable settings.json. Existing buggy installs are detected as already_present so re-running install does not double-add. New regression test (test_detects_legacy_shape_a_install) locks in the backward-compat path.
- **Rejected:** (a) Removing the hook entirely — no plugin hooks.json registers suggest_iterate, so this would silently delete the feature on every adopted project. (b) Switching the writer's command from CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT to CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR — that is a separate path-resolution bug deferred to its own iterate.

---

### ADR-020: Quote uv-run path placeholders + upgrade legacy hook entries (Shape + command) in place
- **Date:** 2026-05-03
- **Section:** Iterate — bug: suggest_iterate quoted-path + Shape A→B upgrade-in-place
- **Context:** On Windows projects whose path contains spaces (OneDrive-synced "AI Backup - Documents", Windows usernames with spaces, paths under "Program Files"), the suggest_iterate UserPromptSubmit hook installed by /shipwright-adopt and /shipwright-project emitted an unquoted shell command. Builds on ADR-019 which had just fixed the carrier shape (Shape A → Shape B) but left the command literal unquoted.
- **Decision:** Wrap the path in double quotes and prefix uv run with --no-project in (a) _HOOK_COMMAND, (b) the documented Shape B JSON snippets in shipwright-project + shipwright-run SKILL.md, (c) every uv-run-with-placeholder snippet across plugins/*/skills/, plugins/*/agents/, and shared/scripts/ docstrings. The hook installer also upgrades any of six legacy command literals in place across BOTH Shape A (carrier replaced wholesale with canonical Shape B) and Shape B (sub.command rewritten) carriers, reporting via a new upgraded:bool field.
- **Commit:** pending
- **Rationale:** External code review (Gemini + GPT via OpenRouter) flagged that recognition-without-rewrite leaves bugs latent on re-run; empirical 12-combination round-trip (Shape A/B × 6 legacy commands) confirms full upgrade-in-place coverage. Layers cleanly on ADR-019 — the carrier shape and command literal are independent failure modes, both must be fixed.
- **Consequences:** Already-adopted projects are fixed by re-running /shipwright-adopt regardless of whether they had the pre-ADR-019 Shape A entry, the post-ADR-019 Shape B entry with unquoted command, or any partial fix. Documentation snippets the LLM agent renders into shell now teach the right pattern. New project convention: any uv-run shell command with a placeholder path argument must be double-quoted; hook commands installed into target projects must additionally pass --no-project.
- **Rejected:** Generating the hook command via shlex.quote with the resolved plugin path embedded literally — would hard-code the version-numbered cache path and break on plugin upgrade. The ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT} indirection is what lets plugin upgrades work without re-running adopt. Also rejected: sweeping plugins/*/hooks/hooks.json between-phase commands — different blast radius (only fails when the plugin install path contains spaces, today only on Windows usernames with spaces); follow-up.

---

### ADR-021: Adopt scaffolds .env.local with profile + framework keys (Layer-3 SSoT)
- **Date:** 2026-05-03
- **Section:** Iterate — feature: adopt scaffolds .env.local from validate_env framework set
- **Context:** Brownfield onboarding via /shipwright-adopt never wrote a .env.local even though the framework reads from it (load_shipwright_env in shared/scripts/lib/env.py, which external_review.py and check-external-review-keys.py both depend on). Operators had to invent the file by hand and the Step H handoff didn't tell them what keys mattered.
- **Decision:** Adopt's generate_adoption_artifacts.py now invokes shared/scripts/validate_env.init_env_file(project_root, 'all', profile_dir, include_framework=True) after run_config write and before compliance seeding. validate_env grew a _SHIPWRIGHT_FRAMEWORK_VARS constant (OPENROUTER_API_KEY, GEMINI_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY in the same fallback order as external_review_config.is_external_review_enabled). init_env_file gained an include_framework kwarg (default False — preserves --init CLI semantics), a hard-stop on .gitignore failure (action=skipped), an export KEY= parser branch, and a rich return contract (action, path, vars, framework_keys, missing_keys). SKILL.md gains Step E.5 + Step H Next-steps banner block.
- **Commit:** PENDING
- **Rationale:** Framework keys live in validate_env.py (single hardcoded list mirrored by an AST-based drift-protection test against external_review_config.py) rather than scattered across N profile JSONs, so the LLM-review key set has one home. .gitignore hard-stop matches the existing security invariant: never stage a secrets file in a repo where the ignore rule cannot be enforced. Both external reviewers (Gemini + OpenAI via OpenRouter) reviewed the plan AND the diff; HIGH and MEDIUM findings on AC2/AC6 contract, drift-protection rigor, payload shape, and gitignore append cases were folded back into the spec and tests before commit.
- **Consequences:** Every brownfield-adopted repo now has a single canonical .env.local SSoT for both profile-specific build/deploy keys and the framework-level review keys. Re-running adopt is byte-equal idempotent. Foundation for the v0.4 shipwright-security phase_parameters work — the new secrets surface can read from the same load_shipwright_env() rather than inventing a second source. One incidental fix: corrected pre-existing broken assertion in test_skips_deploy_phase (deploy phase has worked since 2026-03-30 commit 2ae53b4 but the test was never updated).
- **Rejected:** Add LLM keys to every profile JSON (scatters the same three entries across 3+ files, drift hotspot). Run validate_env via subprocess from the SKILL.md (LLM has to remember to invoke; integration tests can't directly assert behavior). Scaffold .env.example as a checked-in convention (no concrete consumer today; deferred). Generic phase=all framework merge for direct CLI users (changes shared semantics; gated behind include_framework instead).

---

### ADR-022: Quote ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT} in plugins/*/hooks/hooks.json

> Parallel-iterate note: numbering 022 (not 021) because ADR-021 was
> reserved for Sven's parallel `iterate/adopt-env-local-scaffold`
> (env.local scaffolding) which landed first locally.

- **Date:** 2026-05-03
- **Section:** Iterate — bug: hooks.json quoting (deferred from ADR-020)
- **Context:** Every plugins/*/hooks/hooks.json embeds uv-run/bash with unquoted ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}. Same shell word-splitting bug as ADR-020 (suggest_iterate UserPromptSubmit hook), but trigger is the plugin-install-path containing spaces — today only fires for users whose Windows username has a space (e.g. 'John Doe' → ~/.claude/plugins/cache/shipwright/... = C:\Users\John Doe\...). Empirically reproduced via end-to-end shell test: unquoted path → uv exits 2 with 'Failed to spawn: c:\Users\you\My'. Deferred from ADR-020 with explicit out-of-scope marker; closed here.
- **Decision:** Repo-wide sweep of plugins/*/hooks/hooks.json (12 files, ~78 command strings): wrap ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/path/to/script in escaped double quotes inside the JSON value. Add shared/tests/test_hooks_json_quoting.py as parametrized regression that walks every plugin's hooks.json and asserts the AC-1 invariant — future hooks added without quoting fail the test, not the user's machine. NOT adding --no-project: between-phase hooks need uv to resolve plugin-local pyproject.toml deps.
- **Commit:** pending
- **Rationale:** Empirical: end-to-end test reproduces the exact failure (exit 2 + 'Failed to spawn: c:\Users\you\My') and verifies the canonical fix (exit 0). Same as ADR-020 TEST 1, applied to the between-phase hooks.
- **Consequences:** Users with Windows usernames containing spaces (or anyone with a non-default plugin install path containing spaces) get functional Stop/SessionStart/PreToolUse/UserPromptSubmit hooks. Test prevents regression. Diff is purely text — no behavior change for users on space-free install paths.
- **Rejected:** Adding --no-project to between-phase hooks: rejected because they need pyproject.toml resolution for plugin-local deps. Rejected: 'wait until a user reports it' — 5-min mechanical fix with regression test, low risk.

---

### ADR-023: Detect Git-Bash MSYS path-mangling in changelog drop bullets
- **Date:** 2026-05-03
- **Section:** Iterate — bug: changelog MSYS path-mangling linter
- **Context:** During v0.15.0 release prep, an Added bullet appeared as 'C:/Program Files/Git/shipwright-adopt now scaffolds ...' instead of '/shipwright-adopt now scaffolds ...'. Caught at dry-run by hand. Root cause: Git-Bash on Windows auto-converts a leading-slash argv arg into the Bash install root before the receiving Python script sees it (verified locally: 'python -c "print(sys.argv[1])" /foo' → 'C:/Program Files/Git/foo'). aggregate_changelog.py was blind to this and would have published the mangled bullet verbatim.
- **Decision:** Add a release-time linter to aggregate_changelog.py that flags drop-file bullets whose first line starts with the regex '^C:/Program Files/Git/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+(?=[\s/]|$)'. Default behavior: stderr WARN per finding, aggregation continues. New --strict flag (also exposed on the CLI) elevates to AggregatorError + non-zero exit, recommended for release CI. write_changelog_drop.py also emits a defensive WARN at write-time so the operator can catch it earlier. NEITHER auto-rewrites the bullet — legitimate references to paths under Git's install dir would be false positives.
- **Commit:** pending
- **Rationale:** Empirical: bug class confirmed by rerunning 'python -c "..." /foo' locally during this iterate. Detection-without-rewrite is the safer default per the 'verify reviewer findings' memory rule (auto-rewrite risks operator-intended path references). The two-layer defense (write + aggregate) ensures the operator gets at least one chance to catch it even if WARN scrolls off.
- **Consequences:** Future releases catch the bug at aggregator time (or earlier at write time) instead of needing manual dry-run inspection. CI can opt into hard-fail via --strict. New JSON output field msys_mangled_findings exposes the data programmatically. Backward compatible: existing callers without --strict see only added stderr lines on hit.
- **Rejected:** Auto-rewrite at write time: false positives on legitimate paths like 'C:/Program Files/Git/cmd/git.exe ...'. Detect at the shell level via shell wrapper: out of Python's reach. Block the bug class entirely by quoting argv differently inside the Iterate skill: the skill is a textual prompt, not a shell wrapper, can't fix the operator's local Bash. Hard-fail by default: would break existing release automation; opt-in via --strict matches Sven's autonomy preference.

---

### ADR-024: Boundary Tests Foundation
- **Date:** 2026-05-03
- **Section:** Iterate — feature: boundary tests foundation (campaign iterate-skill-hardening Sub-Iterate A)
- **Context:** The 2026-05-03 env-iterate shipped two latent producer/consumer bugs (UTF-8 BOM + inline-comment stripping) that survived 47 unit tests AND two external LLM reviews. Each side's tests passed against a stub representation; drift between sides was invisible until a real round-trip probe surfaced it.
- **Decision:** Encode the lesson as a first-class iterate concept: new touches_io_boundary risk flag in classify_complexity.py (with diff-driven helper is_io_boundary_change), new references/boundary-probes.md (8 edge-case categories), new references/round-trip-tests.md (producer→file→consumer pattern + duplicated-consumer drift protection), 7th Self-Review item Affected Boundaries, Boundary Probe sub-step in Build TDD (Path A 6a, Path B 6a, Path C 5), Phase Matrix row, Override Classes Safety-enforced.
- **Commit:** PENDING
- **Rationale:** Sven caught the env-iterate bugs by demanding an empirical producer→file→consumer round-trip — the cheap fix would have been a one-off test, but the systemic fix is to make every future iterate ask the boundary question up-front. Self-adopting the rule into the skill keeps it from being a tribal-knowledge gotcha.
- **Consequences:** Future iterates touching .env, hooks.json, settings.json, *_config.json, *_state.json (or matching producer/consumer keywords) get the boundary-probe gate automatically. Risk-floor min_complexity=small with enforces=round_trip_test. Path-match covers all known real-world cases; AST-pair detection deferred.
- **Rejected:** Add a generic round-trip mantra to Self-Review without taxonomy support — too easy to skip; risk-flag-driven gating ensures the prompt itself surfaces when probing is mandatory. AST-pair detection now — deferred per Implementation Plan because path-match catches all known cases empirically.

---

### ADR-025: Confidence Calibration phase added to iterate skill (ADR-025)
- **Date:** 2026-05-03
- **Section:** Iterate — feature: confidence calibration phase (Sub-Iterate B)
- **Context:** Confidence collapses without empirical anchor. On the 2026-05-03 env-iterate, 'are you confident?' was answered 'yes' twice — and twice a probe afterwards found a real bug. Three rounds established the asymptote: not done until at least one probe finds nothing.
- **Decision:** Add Step 7.5 Confidence Calibration to Path A between Self-Review and Full Code Review. Mandatory at medium+, Safety-enforced at small with touches_io_boundary, Advisory otherwise. Backed by references/confidence-anti-patterns.md (anti-pattern + asymptote + decision rule + cross-refs).
- **Commit:** PENDING
- **Rationale:** The 'are you confident?' question is unfalsifiable — same brain that wrote the bug being asked if it sees the bug. Replacing the question with named probes converts confidence-attestation into evidence-of-exhaustion at constant marginal cost (one probe ~5min vs hotfix cost).
- **Consequences:** Iterate runs at medium+ now have a structural calibration gate before F0. Self-attestation ('yes I'm confident') replaced by 4 named probes + findings in the iterate spec. Drift-protection tests (test_confidence_anti_patterns_doc.py + test_skill_phase_matrix.py) catch accidental section/row removal.
- **Rejected:** Generic 'review checklist' addition — too soft, no asymptote enforcement. Hard mandatory at all complexity levels — too heavy for trivial markdown edits where no producer/consumer pair exists.

---

### ADR-026: Multi-Session Discipline (ADR-026)
- **Date:** 2026-05-03
- **Section:** Iterate — feature: multi-session discipline (campaign iterate-skill-hardening Sub-Iterate C)
- **Context:** Two Claude Code sessions running in parallel against the same repo (main + .worktrees/<slug>) can race commits/pushes. The 2026-05-03 env-iterate demonstrated this: a non-canonical commit was created locally even though the canonical side had already announced it would integrate the fix. Phrasing read as open invitation, was actually zustimmung-check.
- **Decision:** Add session-role machinery: shared/scripts/lib/session_role.py (read/write/detect, atomic idempotent writes), shared/scripts/checks/check_session_role.py (push guardrail, exit 1 for secondary without SHIPWRIGHT_SECONDARY_PUSH_AUTH=1), SKILL.md B1c phase (parallel-session detection + designation prompt), B1a session-role addendum (secondary-by-default rule), F11 push gate (one-line check).
- **Commit:** PENDING
- **Rationale:** Session-role marker is a JSON producer/consumer boundary by ADR-024 taxonomy — round-trip tests + edge-case probes are mandatory. Atomic write (tmp.replace) mirrors autonomous_loop.py. Idempotent rewrite (same role + worktree → no-op) preserves audit trail set_at + set_by_session_id from the original write. Default permissive on missing marker reflects industry pattern for opt-in guardrails.
- **Consequences:** Future parallel iterates structurally avoid the race: secondary sessions get exit-code-1 at F11 push unless explicitly authorised. Default permissive on missing marker preserves single-session UX (most projects). 29 new tests in shared/tests/ cover round-trip + 8 boundary probe categories that apply to JSON markers (BOM, CRLF, non-ASCII, empty, idempotency, garbage bytes, unknown role, exit-code matrix).
- **Rejected:** File locking (concurrent writes from two processes): out of scope; the discipline IS the locking story (canonical writes, secondary reads). Hard-fail on missing marker: would gate every single-session project unnecessarily. Read role from env var instead of file: file persists across session restarts, env vars do not.

### ADR-027: Boundary Coverage Report
- **Date:** 2026-05-03
- **Section:** Test — feature: boundary coverage report observability tool (campaign iterate-skill-hardening Sub-Iterate D)
- **Context:** Sub-Iterate A (ADR-024) made `## Affected Boundaries` a first-class section in iterate specs and `is_io_boundary_change()` a first-class detector for the diff-driven `touches_io_boundary` risk flag. Without an audit hook, future iterates can silently skip the new section even when their commits touch IO files — drift the campaign was meant to prevent. D closes the loop by scanning iterate-spec history retrospectively.
- **Decision:** New tool `plugins/shipwright-test/scripts/tools/boundary_coverage_report.py` with `scan_specs`, `correlate_with_commits`, `render_markdown`, `render_json`, plus CLI. Wired into `/shipwright-test` via `--report-boundary-coverage` flag (Step 3.95 in test SKILL.md). Markdown table parser is the load-bearing boundary — all 8 probe categories from A's `boundary-probes.md` apply (BOM, CRLF, non-ASCII, alignment markers, whitespace, empty section, exact heading discipline, missing section). Drift signal fires when spec lacks `## Affected Boundaries` BUT either matched commits' `changed_files` OR spec text mentions IO-pattern files.
- **Commit:** PENDING
- **Rationale:** Read-only tool (no destructive writes outside `.shipwright/test-reports/` and `shipwright_test_results.json` merge), so failure modes are output-quality issues not safety issues. Path-match heuristic for round-trip detection is intentionally conservative (false-negatives, never false-positives). Reuses A's `is_io_boundary_change` directly (single source of truth for IO patterns; if A's patterns evolve D inherits them).
- **Consequences:** Smoke-test against live tree finds 17 specs scanned, 4 with `## Affected Boundaries` (= A/B/C/D), 7 drift signals on pre-A iterate specs (env-scaffold, hooks-quoting, suggest-iterate, performance-budgets) — exactly the audit hook intent. 19 new tests in `plugins/shipwright-test/tests/test_boundary_coverage_report.py`; iterate plugin's 146 tests + shared 1237 tests remain green (34 pre-existing failures in `test_phase_plugin_hooks_consistency.py` predate this campaign).
- **Rejected:** AST-pair detection on iterate-spec source files (deferred per A's same-rationale; path-match catches all 7 real drift cases). HTML table parser fallback (no real-world specs use HTML tables; fix forward if observed). Strict header-token validation (`Producer | Consumer | Format` exact-match) (rejected — too brittle against legitimate prose variations like `Producer (writes)` from A's own spec).

### ADR-028: Review-Driven Hardening (campaign iterate-skill-hardening Sub-Iterate E)
- **Date:** 2026-05-03
- **Section:** Iterate / Test / Shared — fix: 6 HIGH + 6 MEDIUM findings from per-sub-iterate code reviews + external_review.py + holistic external review on the campaign
- **Context:** After A/B/C/D shipped locally, retroactive code-reviewer subagents (4×) + `external_review.py --mode code` (4×) + 1 holistic external review surfaced 6 HIGH findings (4 empirically verified by reading shipped code) and ~12 MEDIUMs. The HIGH-severity items split into two groups: (a) real bugs (HIGH-2 worktree-blindness in `detect_parallel_sessions`; HIGH-4 missing test-runner integration for the boundary coverage report; HIGH-5 unscoped round-trip heuristic; HIGH-6 short-slug commit-correlation collisions); (b) spec/code disagreements where the shipped code was correct and the spec text was outdated (HIGH-1 AST-pair AC; HIGH-3 CLI signature). The campaign's dog-food meta-directive is the reason the reviews existed at all — A through D each declared the discipline that surfaces each other's bugs, but the runner contract didn't enforce review invocation, so the gaps weren't caught until human-driven post-ship reviews. Sub-Iterate F closes that runner-contract gap.
- **Decision:** Review-driven fixes only — no scope expansion. The 12 implementation steps in E's spec are the entire surface area. Real bugs are fixed empirically (HIGH-2 uses `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, HIGH-5 scopes the heuristic to `changed_files ∩ test_*.py`, HIGH-6 requires `len(slug) >= 8` OR `\b{slug}\b` regex). Spec/code disagreements are resolved by amending the spec to match the shipped code with a `(deferred)` label + cross-reference to ADR-028 (HIGH-1 AST-pair) or by recording the richer shipped CLI as the canonical interface (HIGH-3). MEDIUM-A1 tightens the `RISK_TAXONOMY` regex patterns from bare verb prefixes (`parse_`, `load_`, `write_`, `\bdump\b`, `serialize`) to anchored function names + qualified stdlib calls (`\bparse_env\b`, `\bjson\.dump(s)?\b`, etc.). MEDIUM-C1 extracts the inline `python -c` snippets in B1c into shared CLI helpers (`shared/scripts/tools/detect_parallel_sessions.py` + `write_session_role.py`) so adopted target projects don't depend on a literal `shared/scripts/lib` path. MEDIUM-C2 makes `write_role`'s tmp file race-safe by construction via `tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile`. MEDIUM-D1 adds `--changed-files` to `record_event.py` (consumed by D's drift detection and HIGH-5's scoping). All changes carry tests.
- **Commit:** PENDING
- **Rationale:** Each fix has a concrete failure mode that the review surfaced empirically — these are not speculative hardening, they are confirmed bugs or load-bearing drift. The path-match-only deferral for AST-pair detection (HIGH-1) is justified by the empirical record: every known real-world boundary bug (the env-iterate's BOM + inline-comment) touched `.env` files in the diff, so the AST-pair complexity is not justified. The "unknown" tri-state for `round_trip_tested` (HIGH-5 fallback) preserves audit signal when legacy events lack `changed_files` rather than silently falsing them. The two-step Option A / Option B documentation for HIGH-4 keeps the API ergonomic (single `--merge-into` for typical use, separate `merge_boundary_coverage.py` helper for the standalone-artefact case).
- **Consequences:** 30 new tests added (iterate +17, test plugin +14, shared session_role +2, shared session_role_cli +3, record_event +6 — net +28 across the four runs after backing out re-counted ones). All previously green tests stay green: iterate 163 (was 146), test plugin 136 (was 122), shared 1240 of 1249 (the same 9 unrelated `test_artifact_path_canon`/`test_gitignore_canon` failures predate this campaign). Risk taxonomy is now meaningfully tighter — six negative-probe prompts (`load_user route`, `dump utility`, `parse_query helper`, `serialize method`, `rewrite the page header text`, `dashboard button color`) all return `[]` instead of `[touches_io_boundary]`, while the positive controls (`parse_env`, `json.dumps`, `settings.json`, `hooks.json`, `*_config.json`) still fire. The HIGH-2 empirical worktree probe (real `git worktree add`, write canonical marker in main + secondary marker in worktree, then call `detect_parallel_sessions` from the worktree path) now sees both markers — the bug C admitted in its Confidence Calibration "Edge cases NOT probed" list is closed.
- **Rejected:** Full AST-pair detection (HIGH-1 — deferred per spec). Reverting D's CLI to the original `--output {file|json}` mode-selector design (HIGH-3 — superseded; richer is strictly better). LOW-severity findings (cosmetic, perf-only, dead code) — explicitly out of scope per spec's "What's NOT in scope". File locking in `write_role` (MEDIUM-C2) — the unique-tmp-name pattern is race-safe by construction; locking would add complexity without benefit.
